ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-21-senat-45-6.u332	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! It is a great honor for me to speak after Judge Stanisław Zabłocki. I remember that when I graduated from law school, the judge was always portrayed as a model of a steadfast attorney from the time of the previous system, who then as one of the few decided to serve in the justice system and be a judge of the Supreme Court. That is why it is an extraordinary responsibility for me to speak immediately after the President about the values that you spoke about. Ladies and gentlemen, the Senate has always been famous for being the House of reflection. He has always been famous for the fact that here in the Senate, senators have wondered about the sound of various kinds of regulations, especially those that may have systemic significance, may have consequences for the protection of the rights and freedoms of individuals. I would like to remind you that, even in this current term, when I was only speaking with regard to a specific law, namely the law on assemblies, when the institution of cyclical assemblies was being introduced, there was a deep discussion in the Senate and, if you remember, even to some extent the harmfulness of the institutions of cyclical meetings was limited. Nevertheless, I am forced to say that what we have seen in recent days is a question of breaching democratic standards in the context of the process and work on the Supreme Court Act. This is a law of systemic importance, a law that can be seen as changing the order of the Republic without changing the constitution and all the more deserving of reflection. After all, we all learned about the existence of this law on the previous Wednesday in the evening. I, realizing the importance of this law, felt that I had to do everything in my power, and did not pay attention to certain private difficulties to participate in this debate, because I know that this is important for citizens. Moreover, this is important because it is not just about the Supreme Court Act. We know very well that in the package we are discussing the law on the National Council of Courts and the law on the system of common courts. In the case of those laws, I held a position... calling for them not to be adopted in this form, as they also pose a threat to the independence of the courts. And all three laws, if you look at them together, can have the effect that they deprive citizens of their right to an independent court. They can lead to the deprivation of one of the most important civil rights, a right without which other rights cannot be defended, control the authorities or impose restrictions. What is the essence of the right to court is the independence of the judiciary. As President Zabłocki said, this is one of the three authorities that are supposed to balance and control each other, but that is why we have an independent judiciary, so that when there is an attempt to restrict civil rights and freedoms, it is the courts that can react. It is therefore important that the courts are independent from politicians, powerful businessmen, large companies of the Treasury, local barons, political agreements. And that's why in the Constitution, given these difficult times, this is exactly how it was shaped. Let us remind you that the preamble to the Constitution contains one very important passage; from this I always start my lectures for law students, for high school students. The passage reads: "Remembers the bitter experiences of the time when fundamental freedoms and human rights were broken in our Homeland, that is, all these constitutional guarantees, art. 180, 179 and more, are to ensure that the same things that have happened in the past do not happen. After all, the preamble also says: "Wanting to guarantee civil rights forever, I stress the words "wanting forever." So we are a reminder of bitter experiences, political processes, various types of abuse, various types of use of justice for political purposes, and that is why we create all these guarantees of the independence of the judiciary and the independence of judges so that these situations from the past do not happen. In order for there to be a real right of defence to be able to read the file of the case and, above all, that the judge who is ruling should be fully independent both internally and externally, i.e. in terms of the external perception of the judge. So that this judge would never be subject to a free mind so that he could be guided only by a constitution and by laws. If we look at the contents of the Supreme Court Act in this context, we see a whole series of allegations. The allegations that I made both in the Sejm and in the opinion that I briefed yesterday at the Senate Committee meeting. This main, most important charge comes down to the de facto retirement of the judges of the Supreme Court, i.e. the resting state, and the verification of those who remain, who are considered to be persons who should continue to work or who may be of some trust... On whose side? On the political side, because, of course, the President will point out at the end of who is to remain, but the whole process is determined in such a way that it is the Minister of Justice, the Attorney General, which is also the party to judicial proceedings, that initiates the process. The role of the National Judicial Council is really about the role of a relay, not an institution that has some kind of guarantee function. This is the most important charge, because in a democratic state it cannot be that the law shortens the term of the Supreme Court judges or any other court. There was an example of Judge Scalia. The Parliament discussed the competences of the President of the United States. Let us remember that in the United States the Supreme Court judge is a judge for the rest of his life, unless he himself, for example. for health reasons, he'll give up. The whole idea of the independence of the Supreme Court is based on the fact that the judges are leaving and it can be said that these windows appear when the President of the United States, of course, taking into account the role of the Congress, is able to appoint this one judge, two times who may change some way in which this court is ruling. But it's a certain icon that's stable, it's the same icon. Like the building of our Supreme Court in Krasiński Square. As far as changes in the judiciary are concerned, there is much debate about the fact that this may violate international standards. If we look at the case law, this recent case law of international courts... I can think of two cases in which issues have been raised, one can say, to deprive judges of office in advance of time for political reasons. The first case is the Commission v Hungary, decided by the Court of Justice of the European Union. It is a matter of the time when the provisions of the Law reducing retirement age were adopted, and suddenly it turned out that judges in Hungary were to retire at the age of 62. It was not without reason that more than 200 judges were involved, especially those who were in charge of the judiciary. This ended with the decision of the Court of Justice of the European Union, but it has not stopped the changes that have taken place, because the fact that the European authorities later rule does not mean that this stops the process of the changes that are taking place. The second example is the case of the judge, the President of the Hungarian Supreme Court, Mr András Baki. András Baka was also appointed for a term of several years; this term was to end much later than it ended. However, in the meantime, the constitution has been changed and certain qualification requirements have been introduced into the Organic Law on the Court of Justice to act as the President of the Supreme Court, which the Supreme Court has changed its name to Kúria, only so that András Baka cannot become the new President of the Supreme Court... sorry that he cannot continue his mission in this position. Once again, the case ended with the resolution of the Grand Chamber of the European Court of Human Rights, which includes 17 judges of the Court of Justice in Strasbourg. Well, since in the meantime these changes in the judiciary have been made and since this exchange of the President has actually taken place. As far as the Supreme Court Act is concerned, it is worth remembering these international standards, it is worth remembering that such methods, when the act deprives judges of the possibility of continuing their duties, are simply incompatible with the principles of the rule of law. They're also against our constitution. Art. The 180th Constitution, which states that changes in the system of courts can be made, must be read in full. It consists of 5 individual guarantees, but As the Senate Legislative Office pointed out in this opinion, which was presented yesterday All these standards need to be looked at together. It cannot be that this reorganisation is being carried out and in this way all the guarantees of non-transferability or non-removalability of judges are removed. Moreover, the question arises as to whether we are dealing with a change in the system of courts or only with a reorganisation within the Supreme Court, i.e. an action which is intended to serve the purpose of changing the internal organization, which allows for all these decisions concerning persons. In my opinion, this kind of reorganisation is contrary to Article. Point (b) of Article 4 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 5. Moreover, granting the Minister of Justice various powers I'm not getting into the details anymore. That were the subject of some reflection... In turn, the question arises as to whether this falls within the guarantees of the article. 149 constitutions. This is the rule that speaks of government departments. After all, the Minister of Justice has his part in the process of appointing judges, because by virtue of the Constitution he is a member of the National Council of Courts. It is next to the president of the NSA, the first president of the Supreme Court, MPs, senators. This is one of the members of the National Judicial Council. And to this, the Constitution has brought the role of Minister of Justice here, not that the Minister of Justice should point out whether he represents these candidacys to remain in the Supreme Court. It's very important. And it's obvious. That the first term of office of the Supreme Court cannot be interrupted by the law. Well, the constitution clearly says it's a six-year term. And just as it is not possible to interrupt the term of office of the National Judicial Council under the law, the first term of office of the Supreme Court cannot be interrupted either. I would like to stress that under the law there are such situations that, on the one hand, judges are sent to rest and, on the other hand, there are a number of possibilities to delegate to serve as judges, to act as judges of the general court with at least a 10-year period of employment in order to carry out their duties. And this is a kind of paradox: on the one hand, the dismissal of judges, their retirement, and on the other hand, the secondment of new ones. It's not only that it violates constitutional standards, it's just as simply irrational in the world. Moreover, it may be doubtful whether the institution of delegation of a judge is responsible for this. If we look at the literature that tells us why there is an institution for posting a judge, we will see that its function is quite different than the response to sudden situations caused by the legislator himself. It is extremely important to me that the Supreme Court Act provides for far-reaching consequences for the employees of the Supreme Court. Last night, after returning from the senate committee meeting, I was very pleased with the position of the NSZZ.Solidarity, because NSZZ...Solidarity took in defense of the employees of the Supreme Court. So it can't be in a democratic state so that under the law the relationship of work is de facto extinguished and it says, "You were employees, you're not going through automatically, now we're going to offer you new conditions for pay and work. It violates Art. 60 constitutions. Art. 60 constitutions speak of the right of equal access to public service. If the legislator does not specify the criteria, does not indicate who and for what reasons he may join, may be appointed for a position, then this standard is violated. And those criteria aren't in the bill at all. Moreover, there are no guarantees that we have discussed, those guarantees against the dismissal of persons who may have this particular employment relationship or may be in this particular protective period. And I am talking about this not only in terms of protecting the interests of these very employees of the Supreme Court, but also of the employees of the National Judicial Council, they, 60 people, are in exactly the same situation. I have presented an opinion, this has not been taken into account in the legislative process. But I am also saying this because the reform of the Customs Service and the tax administration and the establishment of the National Tax Administration have caused exactly the same for customs officers. To me, the Ombudsman now receives complaints from customs officers: Mr. Ombudsman, do something because we have no protection, and suddenly we have been deprived of our posts, because the reform was based on this extinction. This is breaking a certain tradition of how you make personal changes in government administration or in various government offices. And you shouldn't just do that because it's against the constitution. I would also like to point out that when considering critical remarks, of course it is necessary to discuss whether this change will lead to more effective proceedings before the Supreme Court. Much has been said here. I'd just like to point out the numbers. If there are 83 judges currently in court and 44 judges in the Chamber of Private Law and the Chamber of Public Law and 12 disciplinary judges after the change, then 44 will hardly speed up proceedings. What's more, please note Every judge has a paper, which is a set of cases that are currently on his desk. If there's a change and a verification of whether or not there's a release, or a retirement of judges, then someone's gonna have to take over. At that time, regardless of all these constitutional issues, it will simply result in further chronicity in the proceedings and major chaos as regards the functioning of the Supreme Court, especially during the transitional period. I would also like to point out that there are reservations about the establishment of a Disciplinary Chamber. I have reservations, even going away from these issues, whether it is necessary at all, and entering a certain level of reflection on whether the Disciplinary Board is to have an informational, budgetary, management and separate remuneration rules, because the Act provides for all of this. Please note That's what your chief legislator pointed out, too. That the situation of the Disciplinary Chamber is perfectly regulated in this Law on the Supreme Court, with different types of rules separate, for example. The House of Public Law has hardly given attention at all, there has been no reflection on what it will really do. I believe that granting this 40% wage allowance is not just a matter of breaching a certain standard of operation of the Supreme Court, i.e. that it does not stand out financially for work, but it is simply a violation of the constitutional principle that judges should be rewarded in a manner consistent with the dignity of the office and the scope of their duties. I stress that the constitution in the article. Point (a) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 2 speaks of the scope of their duties. Statistics do not suggest that the Disciplinary Board should have more cases than the other two chambers. So that we should pay attention to this too. I would also like to draw attention to this issue, whether, at times, on the occasion of the amendment, the adoption of this law and the introduction of amendments so-called. The presidential situation didn't happen, I'd say, rather unusual. Well, the analysis I have presented shows that there is a strong likelihood that, at a time when it comes to the clinch, when it comes to the appointment of members of the judges of the National Judicial Council, I mean this majority of 3/5, the decision-making authority can actually take over one of the assemblies of this new National Judicial Council. I remind you that the Act on the National Judicial Council states that the Council will have 2 meetings, including one so-called. political, consisting of 4 Members, 2 Senators, the President of the NSA, the First President of the Supreme Court and the Minister of Justice. And nothing's gonna change here because these people are... Well, maybe the president, the first president of the Supreme Court, will change, God forbid. And this other chamber may remain uncalled for for a certain time. Then it's called. The political assembly of the National Judicial Council will identify these candidates. This is, I think, some danger or maybe some mistake. The question is whether or not it is intended It is worth paying attention to, because I am not convinced that the President really meant it, regardless of the fact that I disagree that politicians choose the members of the judges of the National Judicial Council. The findings of the Round Table, the results of the work of the Undertable for Justice, the works in which Jarosław Kaczyński and Adam Strzembosz participated, were such that the judges should elect the members of the National Council of Courts. Of course, what also raises reservations is that the infringement of all these standards will have consequences from the point of view of European law, the consequences of the fact that there may be a charge as to whether, in fact, national courts which are not independent of political authorities are still Community courts, or whether judgments of these courts will be recognised if they are not independent courts. I do not agree with the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office that this is a matter which does not fall within the scope of European law. It is precisely because every court of a Member State is also a European court, i.e. it must interpret and apply European law that we are dealing with this situation. It is worth noting that such solutions are simply contrary to the Article. 47 Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, i.e. with guarantees of the right to court. These are the main charges. Of course, we could go into further details. Well, for example, yesterday at the Senate Committee meeting we learned that the Minister of Justice will agree whether employees, such as ordinary ones, of the Supreme Court can be employed somewhere outside the Supreme Court, e.g. teaching at the university. Well, it's kind of like I'm asking Marshal Sejm and Senate if my employees... like my office employees are asking Marshal Sejm and Senate if they can be employed outside the office of the spokesperson. It's neither rational nor thoughtful. And it is not related to a certain nature of service, and it is the first President of the Supreme Court that is simply an employer for these people. I would like to stress that there are a lot of these comments that we really... Every reading of the bill indicates that... we find out what's in it, we may also find out about some hidden intentions. Today's words by Minister Warchoł were quite interesting to me that this law would probably mean changes in the composition of the State Election Commission. It is therefore hardly surprising that this law raises a number of doubts and voices of opposition. And I would like to stress that many organizations and institutions protest against the Supreme Court Act. They are also national, Polish NGOs, and professional authorities, and some trade unions. There is also the position of the President of the European Parliament, Secretary-General of the Council of Europe. Today I learned, literally just now, that even the head of the Human Rights Committee in the OSCE Parliamentary Assembly also expressed his negative opinion. It was very interesting to me. and may not have been seen by the public What happened at the meeting of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights of the Sejm. I do not know whether the senators recall that a representative of the OSCE, the OSCE's Office of Democratic Institutions and Human Rights, with whom the Senate regularly cooperates, has been present for a moment. For, for example, the rights of assembly were regularly ordered and discussed by the OSCE representatives. And the OSCE representative said with some honesty that the OSCE is ready to prepare an opinion at the request of the Supreme Court and it will take from 4 to 6 weeks. Well, that's what it takes. Because it's such a big deal, something like this doesn't happen in two or three days or one week, it just takes reflection. And it's hard to be surprised. And I would like to stress that today, among these voices of reflection, there have also been voices from representatives of institutions in other countries. This morning I received a letter from a well-known Czech dissident, close associate of Václav Havel, who is currently acting as Ombudsman, Anna Šabatova. It formulated it in its own name and in the name of the four representatives of the Supreme Courts. And I would like to read you this letter because I think it is extremely moving. The letter is entitled "We cannot remain silent." Rzeczpospolita Poland and its people are for us a permanent and inseparable part of the community of the peoples of Central and Eastern Europe, which has shared a common destiny for centuries. The heroism of the Polish nation during World War II, its resistance to both the dictatorship of the former Soviet Union and national authoritarian practices, as well as its effective reconstruction of state sovereignty and the device of the democratic system and the rule of law after 1989 were a significant and lasting inspiration for us. Over the past year, however, we have witnessed a process with our close neighbour that threatens the very basis of the principles on which the democratic rule of law is based. After paralyzing the Constitutional Tribunal and subjugating the public media of the current party policy in the last year, an unprecedented attack on the independence of the Polish judiciary occurs in these days. The laws adopted on the National Council of Courts, on common courts and the law on the Supreme Court, which enable the executive and legislative authorities to completely deprive the judiciary of its independence, the inconvenient judges of office and to subdue the judicial authority of political leadership, are an attack on the very foundations of the democratic rule of law. Even though we are aware of the sovereignty of the Polish state and respect for it, we cannot remain silent about the steps that threaten its very source, which are the inviolable values of European civilization, humanism and fundamental rights and freedoms. The letter was signed by Pavel Rychetský, President of the Constitutional Tribunal, Pavel Šámal, President of the Supreme Court, Josef Bax, President of the NSA, Pavel Zeman, Attorney General, and finally Anna Šabatová, Ombudsman of the Czech Republic. I would like to stress that such voices are not voices of opposition. Here it is clearly emphasized: we respect sovereignty. But these are the voices of concern for the future of our country. Are we gonna be the same partner? Aren't we squandering our work? Will we be a predictable, stable, reform-oriented country, but a dedicated idea that it is not possible to break the law that the process of amending the law is subject to the highest standards? Because if the legislative process is violated, we will be violating the rights of the weakest. Right, right, right, right, right, Perhaps now, in this Supreme Court, there are pregnant girls, assistant judges who are worried about whether they will have a job. And the Minister's assurances won't help them if suddenly for some reason it turns out that they are not further desirable in the Supreme Court. But the fact that they are being put in such a situation should not, in my opinion, take place in a democratic state. But these letters, these positions, this is also a concern about whether, as a Member State of the European Union, we will be able to be treated as a party that can be trusted. I would like to emphasise: all our country's cooperation within the European Union is based on the principle of mutual trust. We have no recognition of judgments unless we have mutual trust in each other. We don't have judicial cooperation in criminal, civil, family matters, if that trust in us doesn't exist. And this could be about criminal matters, when we want to acquire, bring to Poland and judge the perpetrator of the crime, who, using the freedom of movement of people, lived somewhere in Majorca. This can be about caring matters when we deal with parents who are divorced, one lives in France, the other lives in Poland. And there must be cooperation here, it cannot be that we, by questioning the principle of the independence of the courts, will at the same time lead to this principle of mutual recognition of judgments and cooperation in matters being called into question. As an ombudsman, I stand by the rights and freedoms of citizens. I have to worry, and I have to alarm myself when there may be changes in the law that may be difficult for citizens and which may cause inequality for citizens, especially in the context of access to judicial procedures. And as I wonder about my practice, my daily practice, the many cases that go to the Ombudsman's office, the cases where citizens often have a state on the other side of the country in its various lives, I keep thinking about what all these changes will have to do with this citizen's situation. And here I'm not just talking about the Supreme Court, I'm talking about the exchange of court presidents, faculty presidents, the creation of different rules that apply to the selection of judges, for example. that if there is a court president, he has a big influence on which judge will be on duty. These could all be situations that will have very serious consequences for citizens sooner or later. Let us consider, for example, matters that are the subject of a public debate. If we're dealing with a victim of police violence, a victim of police torture, can this victim count on a full, decent, thorough explanation of the case, quality control, and how the case was explained by the prosecution? Does he have a full right to be compensated if this is a matter of great political interest? There was a lot of talk about the fact that justice is only for the poor... sorry, only for the rich, and various changes will be made to strengthen the poor. Ladies and gentlemen, I have recently presented a report on the use of torture in police stations, saying: let us resolve this problem by ensuring that a lawyer is present at the police station. Why? Because this rich guy's gonna be okay, because he's gonna call a trusted lawyer, and the poor guy's not gonna be okay if he doesn't have to be a lawyer at the police station. And just recently I got an answer from the Minister of Justice that he doesn't see the need for change in this area. So these poor people, those excluded, those who are most likely victims of police violence, will not have proper protection. But, of course, we're talking about court here. Yes? Because the question arises whether, if we have matters that politicians will be interested in, in such situations, they will have a full right to court. Come on. If we have the mother of a girl who died in custody because of the negligence of the Prison Service, will she be able to count on full justice in identifying the case, even though the case may be difficult for the prison policy? Will the victim of domestic violence be able to count on the full support of the courts, the sudden response, the isolation of the perpetrator, if the perpetrator can prove to be a politician? Will the inhabitants of the small village be able to obtain compensation for building energy lines on their land in violation of various procedures if it is not in the interest of the energy company? The main energy companies in Poland are state-owned companies. Finally, if we have a whole bunch of foreign currency loan holders, they will be able to count on justice, that at some point the Supreme Court will find that they were used in mass so-called. Abusive clauses, which violate consumer rights, where half of the banking sector currently belongs to the State and it is not in the interest of the State to suddenly reduce the profitability of banks that constantly feed the state budget? Will broadcasters who benefit from radio and television concessions be able to appeal effectively against the penalties imposed by the National Radio and Television Council if they were critical of the government? Will the inhabitants of the municipalities be able to challenge the decision to build a chicken farm, a cattle farm, if the decision is followed by a local politician who has an interest in creating this particular farm, and is it a policy similar to the governing party? And finally, let's ask ourselves a question. We're discussing the employees of the Supreme Court, so let's imagine that this bill will come into force and they'll lose their jobs. So where do they appeal? To the labour court? In the meantime, the president of the court and perhaps the head of the department will change. Will they be able to count on full justice if this is what the legislator has decided? They are already before the courts of the general cases of dismissal of the staff of the Constitutional Tribunal. Fired, you can say, in a fairly similar mode. I am talking about these examples to show that the reform of the justice system will affect the lives of ordinary people. This is not just a problem of politicians, elites, it is not a problem of dispute over whether another policy or wondering what a sentence would be in two years' time if there was some greater political subjection here. Of course, that too, but we must remember that independent courts are the guarantors of the rights and freedoms of ordinary people. And we must always and with all our might emphasize that. I am aware that the courts need reform. Minister Warchoł or Minister Ziobro know very well that I, as spokesperson, conduct regular correspondence on many subjects. The experts of the court, the proceedings in juvenile cases, the scope of mediation, the scope of the cases that the courts deal with... Moreover, I can even say that this dialogue is that there is even a will of determination, but I regret that we are not discussing this very determination today. For example, how to regulate juvenile proceedings, because this has been a problem for years. But we are discussing laws that, in my opinion, have only one purpose, a staff goal. I deeply wish that we would talk about the justice system properly, so that we would not yell at each other so that we would not throw ourselves with various loud arguments, but that we would talk in such a way as to show our attachment to constitutional values, to those values which are part of our civilisational achievements and which are the result of the bitter experiences of the time when human rights were violated in our homeland. These 3 laws, in my opinion, and above all the Supreme Court Act, are of an apparent nature and their purpose is primarily a human purpose. I would like to emphasise here and here the one point that the Minister was talking about. It was also the subject of President Zabłocki's speech. Namely, I would like to refer to an issue which is one of the justifications of the law. So the Minister says: we have to adopt a law, because in this Supreme Court, this resolution of 2007 was adopted, which removed the rules of responsibility of the officers for the communist crimes at risk of being punished by less than 3 years of imprisonment. Ladies and gentlemen, Perhaps I'll surprise you here. I absolutely agree that this resolution is wrong. Moreover, as Minister Warchoł was still an employee of the Ombudsman's Office, he is currently on unpaid leave because of his governmental role Moreover, we talked so effectively that I applied to the Constitutional Tribunal in April 2016. A very comprehensive proposal that just shows why a different solution should be sought here. A proposal likely to restore liability in this respect. And I am waiting for the Constitutional Tribunal to recognize such a proposal, and in this way, you can say, eliminate this resolution of the Supreme Court from circulation. So there's a solution. But there's a case pending at the Court, and I don't see that there's a time limit for finding it, and it could be handled relatively quickly. That's why I can't accept that now one example that really... There's an attempt to make things right... And here this example is used as an argument to justify such a profound reform of the Supreme Court. I would like to emphasise and once again to point out that these changes that are being discussed are changes that change the constitution without a formal constitutional amendment, which are de facto changing the system without having, at the same time, a constitutional majority. The Constitution is in effect. The Constitution was adopted in a referendum, adopted by the whole nation in a referendum and in accordance with the appropriate modalities. The Constitution is the most important document on the basis of which the functioning of our country is based. I have sworn on a constitution and I cannot allow it and I will not agree to make statutory changes in such a way as to de facto distort the content of the constitution. I think it's my duty as an ombudsman to be here and say it. In the end, I would like to share my personal reflection. I started public service when it came to dealing with human rights in 2004. In 2004, as a young lawyer, I started working at the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. I started dealing with a lot of difficult things. I started helping people, I started listening to them, I tried to be maximum apolitical and impartial in my actions. I have dealt with human rights from the very beginning, and in a sense I have grown up in the spirit of everything that older colleagues, people who were either members of the Helsinki Committee wanted to tell me... ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-21-senat-45-6.u346	As far as Senator Rybicki's question is concerned, I would like to point out that art. 45 of the Constitution establishes this guarantee of the right to court. This is not only about the fact that a citizen can turn to the court, but also about the fact that this court is to characterize you, you can say, quality, there must be certain guarantees about how it works, that it is primarily a court, and that the concept of the court is that it is independent, and that the judges who rule are independent. As for independence, we had a speech by Judge Zabłocki, President of Zabłocki, so I would not like to repeat these too. Art. 6 The European Convention on Human Rights reiterates these guarantees. Moreover, we can find a whole range of judgments of the European Court of Human Rights which interpret what the right to court is, what is the length of proceedings, when we can say that the proceedings are chronic or not, when we can say that the court is not impartial. Such an example of interesting judgments are judgments concerning court assessors who were not, you can say, judges in the full sense of the word, and for these reasons this has been undermined. In the context of these two cases, which, of course, are of public interest, I would like to say that these are matters which somewhat symbolize the fact that every citizen can at some point enter into a conflict with the state, a conflict with state institutions. And it could be the seicento driver who might accidentally be on the road, and then there are doubts about who was guilty, and politicians might have a certain interest in settling the case in a certain way. And that is why we have all these procedural guarantees so that this person who finds himself in such a situation will have the guarantee that he will not be unduly detained, that the court will recognize that he will have the right to an attorney, that he will have the right to have access to the case file, that he will be treated equally with the prosecutor, that politicians will not express earlier, in breach of the presumption of innocence, his view of the case, but also that they will not express expectations, what they would expect and what this court is to do. And if we imagine that there is, however, a concentration in one hand of different powers and also an influence on the judiciary, we will see that, unfortunately, there is a great deal of concern as to whether such statements expressed by politicians do not later affect the specific infectiveness of the entire judicial proceedings, or whether there will not be a situation in which the court is filled in such a way that the court is, may be, in a manner appropriate to the case. After all, we all know that judges are only human beings and politicians can well know which judges will be more friendly towards politicians and who will actually be those truly independent judges carrying this independence in their hearts. Same with the death case, the tragic death case at the police station. Well, we've dealt with a situation here before, that some politicians have even publicly expressed their views on the circumstances of this death, who is guilty. So how is it that if the same politician later supervises the way the courts work, this court will find itself? Is the court e.g. it will actually be able to fully resolve the various doubts if the proceedings are terminated or for example. The guilt of the perpetrators will be classified not in the context of death, but in the context of torture alone? These are extremely serious matters, because they are translated into a sense of justice. And why is that important? Because if the case is not properly recognised, if the courts do not exercise this proper supervision over how the prosecution is dealing with the case, then the European Court of Human Rights will be held later. The Court in Strasbourg can conclude that the right to life has also been infringed for reasons of the so-called "right to life'. procedural, for such reasons, that the ordinary case in the world has not been properly clarified, and it may turn out that without independent courts, cases will often not be properly clarified. I would like to draw attention to the fact that it is not only about the interests of our citizens, but also about the interests of the Polish state. Let's imagine that we have a person who is suspected of a serious crime, and this person Like I said. He escapes from Poland, gets to Malta, Cyprus, Italy. Our prosecutors are on the move, they are demanding that this person be released on the basis of a European arrest warrant, and the court in Malta says: we will not give up this citizen because there is such political tension around this case that we have no guarantee that you will judge him fairly. And the courts will be able to do it. So the basic instrument for cooperation in criminal matters, which is the European Arrest Warrant, can also be called into question when the independence of the courts is called into question. Because the whole idea is based on mutual recognition and mutual trust. We work together, we create a common area of security and justice, because we recognise that we all have more or less similar standards. This is a little bit on the basis of what I would like to do about handing over, maybe I will go back to the example of a European arrest warrant and question it because of the standards in prisons. So if the conditions in custody violate the prohibition of inhuman and degrading treatment, then you can't hand over the prisoner. This democratic mechanism is extremely stingy, almost none. The facts are that, in order for someone to become a judge, it must be decided by the National Judicial Council. And who decides who's gonna be on the National Judicial Council? The judges themselves decide first and foremost. It can be said that this is such a co-optation, because the judges decide who will become a judge, the judges decide who will be promoted, the judges decide who will be prosecuted for disciplinary action or not, the minister can at best ask for it, bring it forward, the judges decide who will be expelled from his corporation. So the judges decide everything. Where's the democratic control here? The only element of the influence of a democratic state on the process of appointing judges is the moment when the President hands out the act of nomination, but after all, it was the National Council of Judicial Affairs that adopted resolutions in which it marginalized the role of the President, stating that he has in fact only to play the role of notary and that he must always implement the decisions that come to him by virtue of the conclusions of the National Council of Judicial Affairs, a body completely dominated by judges. So these mechanisms of democratic legitimacy, in terms of the Polish judiciary, and the systemic solutions that were adopted as part of the compromise, a compromise at the Round Table with post-communists, between the solidarity side and the communists, unfortunately proved to be very unreliable and questionable precisely from the point of view of democratic legitimacy. There's been a lot of accusations. They have been going through the whole day in discussions, in various speeches That we are violating the principles of three divisions of power. In this case, let me refer to the model of relations between the legislative, executive and judicial authorities in a country which, you can say, is such a model example of building a relationship based on the principles that French lawyer Montesquius has in his time outlined, namely the United States. I believe that each of you has heard that the Supreme Court judges in the United States are being appointed by the President with the approval of the House of Representatives. In that case, if to assume that the participation of a democratic factor, i.e. in that case a president who has democratic legitimacy precisely because he was elected by the people in the general election, that participation through his individual competence to designate a particular person to the Supreme Court is a violation of the principle of three divisions of power, then I ask whether the United States is a country which contradicts this principle of three divisions of power, which is precisely there in the most full expression. The difference is that in Poland this democratic legitimacy of a judge is completely negligible in relation to the democratic legitimacy that operates in the United States. But let's not go this far, to the United States, to this model system solution that's actually referring to the Montesquius principle. The three divisions of power and balance of influence of these authorities at certain moments on each other. As regards model solutions in European countries And they were called here by Minister Warchoł I will appeal to one example, that is to say to the greatest European democracy, Germany. It's about who's gonna be the judge of the Supreme Court. And we're talking about the Supreme Court here today. The Supreme Court judges themselves do not decide, as it is in Poland. Because if someone wanted to become a Supreme Court judge in Poland today, he could only be a Supreme Court judge if and only if they agreed to do so and that was decided by their colleagues from the Supreme Court. Only then. There is no other way and way to reach the Supreme Court if it does not please the Supreme Court judges and if they do not decide that a person could be a Supreme Court judge. What's it like in Germany? Well, it's a completely different pole when it comes to solutions. In Germany, who will be the judge of the Supreme Court, on the one hand, the committee set up by the Bundestag halfway, i.e. by the German parliamentarians with democratic legitimacy, and on the other hand, by the ministers of justice of the land, who, together in secret voting, decide who will be the candidate for the Supreme Court. Moreover, this decision must still have the approval of the German government in the person of the Minister of Justice. So this is, you can say, a very different model of bringing judges to the Supreme Court than the one that applies in Poland, where there is a principle of total cooption, where once again I stress it. The judges themselves decide who they want among their fellow judges. Our proposals contained in this submission, which arouse such controversy and arouse emotions, and are often related to dishonest and false arguments, calculated on the basis of social emotions, do not go as far as in the United States as it is with regard to the process of appointing judges. Nor do they go as far as Germany's appointing judges. We want it to continue to be the National Judicial Council, dominated by judges, to take decisions binding or crucial in the process of appointing judges. We want the judicial recruitment system to be much more democratic and transparent, so that anyone who meets the requirements of being in such an honorable group can aspire to judicial positions and be transparent, transparently assessed. We just want to rule out a cooptation mechanism that currently limits access to the judiciary, as I explained, by narrowing the candidates only to those who were previously anointed by their colleagues from the Supreme Court. So, in short, it's the National Judicial Council in a democratic and transparent, transparent trial that will decide who will be the Supreme Court judge in the future. Of course, a binding final decision will be made by the President, as this And it's happening today. But certainly the process of opinion in the National Judicial Council will be crucial to the final formulation of this decision. So that it is not the government, not the Minister of Justice, as it is in Germany, not the President himself by pointing out a finger, as it is in the United States, but the National Judicial Council in a public competition, transparent, subject to democratic control, with the subsequent participation of the President, will decide who is the Supreme Court judge if of course the person notified passes this process and will comply with the requirements set out in the Act. This position of the Supreme Court led to a mass remission of cases in the Polish prosecutor's office. At that time, the Supreme Court stood in the right position and pointed out that such events should be eligible not only for the exhaustion of an offence prohibited by the Tax Code, but also under the Criminal Code, and therefore more stringent criminal liability is threatened by the perpetrator. I think that this was, and this is, a solution that was perfectly rational and right, because the person who was prejudiced about criminal liability was instructed about it and was able to avoid answering the question. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-21-senat-45-6.u498	Mr. Marshal! High Senate! The statement, made on behalf of the Senators' Group, is addressed to the Prime Minister of the Council of Ministers, Prime Minister Beata Szydło. Dear Prime Minister, We are concerned about the process of including customs in the National Tax Administration. On June 1, we asked the head of the National Tax Administration, but we still have not received an answer. Contrary to government announcements, the law does not eliminate people associated with former security services and sometimes even protects them. But it hits outstanding officers and deprives even pregnant women of their jobs. These cases have been reported, among others. Elbląg. Many officers accept humiliating conditions for re-employment to keep their jobs. It is the effect of a law which, in one move, deprived 14.5 thousand officers of their jobs and gave a handful of people the right to decide freely who to extend their employment contract to, without any means of appeal to the absent workers. We have the right to suspect that many decisions are made for private reasons, not for the sake of service protecting Polish borders. That is why we expect the Prime Minister to react quickly. We expect information on the number of people who have not received a job offer in the CAS by 31 May, on the criteria applied in these cases, on the number and nature of complaints and appeals against the absence of a job proposal or on the transfer to another post and on the response to these complaints and appeals. We expect the Prime Minister to immediately order you to monitor these proceedings, taking into account the problems, individual protests and appeals presented in this statement, as well as the information contained in the positions of various institutions, including the trade union Celnica.pl. We declare the willingness to transfer documents and contacts to those who feel harmed by the conduct of the tax administration in this process. Please inform us of the decisions made. And a statement, also made on behalf of a group of senators, addressed to the Minister of National Defence, Mr Antoni Macierewicz. Dear Minister, In June, the book "Macierewicz" and his mysteries were published. The author of the book Tomasz Friday shows source documented information suggesting that you have numerous, including business, links with former PRL security agents, Russian GRU intelligence services and people associated with the Russian Mafia. The book goes to at least 60 thousand readers, and the information contained in it is reproduced by numerous media. The recipients of this information face numerous questions about the minister who is responsible for the Polish army and national defence. And no Pole can doubt that this minister is an impeccable person, fearing questions about his past and compromising connections. Therefore, we expect that the Minister with energy, with which he is trying to prove that on 10 April 2010 an assassination of the Polish government aircraft took place, will deal with explaining to the Polish public the allegations concerning himself and will publicly explain them. We believe that this is required by the Polish state right. At the same time, we expect that you will not obscure yourself with the announcement by the Minister of Defence of the possibility of committing a crime related to the publication of a book. We look forward to a quick public response to these very serious, discrediting every Pole, and the minister of the Polish government in particular, allegations. The statements were signed by senators: Mieczysław Augustyn, Barbara Borys-Damięcka, Leszek Czarnobaj, Jarosław Duda, Jerzy Fedorowicz, Piotr Florek, Tomasz Grodzki, Maciej Grubski, Wiesław Kilian, Kazimierz Kleina, Władysław Komarnicki, Grzegorz Napieralski, Marian Poślednik, Jadwiga Rotnicka, Jan Rulewski, Waldemar Sługocki, Grażyna Anna Sztark, Przemysław Termiński, Piotr Zientarski, Barbara Zdrojewska and Jerzy Wcisła. And Bogdan Klich also signed a statement addressed to you by the Prime Minister. Thank you very much.
